# $ diff -r prod-html/normalized local-html/normalized
# NOTE: analysis (not part of raw diff -r output): all 9 files register as differing,
# but each is a single minified HTML line so diff -r reports the whole line as changed.
# Root causes identified by isolating the differing substrings (python difflib) after
# scrubbing the site-origin string:
#   1. Site origin: prod pages use https://bcn-services.com in canonical/OG/JSON-LD tags;
#      the local build falls back to http://localhost:3000 (a hardcoded default, not the
#      actual :3999 port used here) — expected env-driven difference, not a regression.
#   2. A handful of short random alphanumeric tokens inside the RSC/hydration payload
#      (Next.js flight-stream boundary ids) differ per build/request — expected
#      non-determinism, not a content or route regression.
# No other content differences were found in the /, /about, /pricing, /services, /work,
# /privacy, /terms pages. robots.txt differs only in the Sitemap: absolute URL (same cause
# as #1). sitemap.xml differs only in the same origin swap plus <lastmod>, which sitemap.ts
# generates as `new Date()` at request/build time (expected, not a route/content regression).

diff -r /Users/nateseluga/bcns/docs/architecture/baselines/2026-09-15/bcns/prod-html/normalized/__.html /Users/nateseluga/bcns/docs/architecture/baselines/2026-09-15/bcns/local-html/normalized/__.html

He is drawn to efficiency, and to software that optimizes your workflow exactly how you want it to. Time spent inside day-to-day operations showed him how much of a workday goes to operations that could be automated but weren&#x27;t, due to the inconvenience of change and hesitation to pay for outside solutions that aren&#x27;t tailored to the existing process. He has extensive experience creating tools to reduce friction in his own work. bcns exists because that toolbox should not stop with the person who built it.</p><div aria-hidden="true" class="mb-[1.125rem] mt-6 h-px bg-border"></div><ul class="mt-auto font-display text-[0.8125rem] text-muted-foreground"><li>Computer science, Harvey Mudd College</li></ul></div><div data-reveal="" class="opacity-0 lift-card flex h-full flex-col rounded-[1.25rem] border border-border bg-card p-8 sm:p-10 sm:px-10 sm:py-11" style="animation-delay:110ms"><div class="flex items-center gap-5"><span aria-hidden="true" class="flex size-[4.5rem] shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full border border-accent bg-secondary font-display text-[1.375rem] font-bold text-primary">BC</span><div><h2 class="text-[1.375rem] font-semibold sm:text-[1.625rem]">Brandon Chung</h2><p class="mt-1 font-display text-[0.8125rem] font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.1em] text-primary">Business &amp; clients</p></div></div><p class="mt-[1.625rem] text-[0.90625rem] leading-[1.75] text-muted-foreground">Brandon studies economics at New York University and has worked in business evaluation and optimization, including time as an investor. That work comes down to one question asked repeatedly: what enables a business to make money, and what is quietly slowing down that process. He has spent time finding the businesses and projects that already work and identifying what would make them work better. On a bcns project he owns scoping and communication, meaning he is the one who advises you on solutions that are worth building.</p><div aria-hidden="true" class="mb-[1.125rem] mt-6 h-px Time spent inside day-to-day operations showed him how much of a workday goes to operations that could be automated but weren&#x27;t, due to the inconvenience of change and hesitation to pay for outside solutions that aren&#x27;t tailored to the existing process. He has extensive experience creating tools to reduce friction in his own work. bcns exists because that toolbox should not stop with the person who built it.</p><div aria-hidden="true" class="mb-[1.125rem] mt-6 h-px bg-border"></div><ul class="mt-auto font-display text-[0.8125rem] text-muted-foreground"><li>Computer science, Harvey Mudd College</li></ul></div><div data-reveal="" class="opacity-0 lift-card flex h-full flex-col rounded-[1.25rem] border border-border bg-card p-8 sm:p-10 sm:px-10 sm:py-11" style="animation-delay:110ms"><div class="flex items-center gap-5"><span aria-hidden="true" class="flex size-[4.5rem] shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full border border-accent bg-secondary font-display text-[1.375rem] font-bold text-primary">BC</span><div><h2 class="text-[1.375rem] font-semibold sm:text-[1.625rem]">Brandon Chung</h2><p class="mt-1 font-display text-[0.8125rem] font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.1em] text-primary">Business &amp; clients</p></div></div><p class="mt-[1.625rem] text-[0.90625rem] leading-[1.75] text-muted-foreground">Brandon studies economics at New York University and has worked in business evaluation and optimization, including time as an investor. That work comes down to one question asked repeatedly: what enables a business to make money, and what is quietly slowing down that process. He has spent time finding the businesses and projects that already work and identifying what would make them work better. On a bcns project he owns scoping and communication, meaning he is the one who advises you on solutions that are worth building.</p><div aria-hidden="true" class="mb-[1.125rem] mt-6 h-px
